3155-// dimensions this app can genuinely enforce: which modules appear in the
3156-// sidebar at all (allowedModules), and whether the role can create/edit/
3157-// delete or only view (writeAccess). This is a real, meaningful two-axis
3158-// permission model — not a full per-action permission matrix. Building
3159-// that would mean gating every individual create/edit/delete control
3160-// across all twenty modules individually, a large, separate undertaking
3161-// documented as a follow-up rather than attempted here at risk of leaving
3162-// half the app's buttons correctly gated and half not. What's below is

3897-// feature. Uses the same global event-bus pattern as notify()/toastBus
3898-// rather than a hook threaded through every mutation site: audit logging
3899-// is a cross-cutting concern, and forcing every function that might need
3900-// to log something to accept and forward an extra parameter would ripple
3901-// through the codebase for no real benefit. Complements the Auditor role
3902:// (see Settings) — that role can see every module, but without an actual
3903-// trail of who did what and when, "seeing everything" wasn't the same as
3904-// being able to audit anything.

3909-// identity. A production audit trail must be written server-side against
3910-// a real authenticated session — a client can log an action, but it can't
3911-// be trusted to honestly report who performed it. This is a UX-layer
3912-// approximation of the real capability, not the capability itself.

4847-// Two genuinely different categories of channel, and the UI says so
4848-// honestly rather than presenting all six as equally real:
4849-//
4850:// Slack and Microsoft Teams both support "incoming webhooks" — a plain
4851-// URL that accepts a POST request with a JSON payload. That's something a
4852-// browser can do directly with fetch(), no server required, so these two
4853-// are wired for real.
4854-//
4855-// Email, SMS, WhatsApp, and Push all require a trusted server holding a
4856-// secret (an SMTP/API key, a Twilio Account SID + Auth Token, Meta's
4857-// WhatsApp Business API credentials, an FCM/APNs server key). None of
4858-// those can ever be safely embedded in client-side code — the same
4859-// principle already documented for the AI Assistant's API key. Building a
4860-// button that pretends to send an email with no backend would be actively
4861-// dishonest, not just incomplete, so these four are shown as real
4862-// configuration screens with a functional=false flag and an explanation,
4863-// not a fake "Sent!" toast.
